- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Uneven Impact of Post-Training Quantization in Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20893v1)
- **Authors**: Benjamin Marie, Atsushi Fujita
- **Abstract**: Quantization is essential for deploying large language models (LLMs) on resource-constrained hardware, but its implications for multilingual tasks remain underexplored. We conduct the first large-scale evaluation of post-training quantization (PTQ) on machine translation across 55 languages using five LLMs ranging from 1.7B to 70B parameters. Our analysis reveals that while 4-bit quantization often preserves translation quality for high-resource languages and large models, significant degradation occurs for low-resource and typologically diverse languages, particularly in 2-bit settings. We compare four quantization techniques (AWQ, BitsAndBytes, GGUF, and AutoRound), showing that algorithm choice and model size jointly determine robustness. GGUF variants provide the most consistent performance, even at 2-bit precision. Additionally, we quantify the interactions between quantization, decoding hyperparameters, and calibration languages, finding that language-matched calibration offers benefits primarily in low-bit scenarios. Our findings offer actionable insights for deploying multilingual LLMs for machine translation under quantization constraints, especially in low-resource settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of post-training quantization (PTQ) on machine translation (MT) performance across 55 languages using five large language models (LLMs) ranging from 1.7B to 70B parameters. It compares four quantization techniques: AWQ, BitsAndBytes (bnb), GGUF, and AutoRound. The study reveals that while 4-bit quantization generally preserves translation quality for high-resource languages and larger models, significant degradation occurs for low-resource and typologically diverse languages, especially at 2-bit precision. It also explores the influence of decoding hyperparameters (temperature and nucleus sampling) and calibration data language on the quantized models' performance. The study finds that GGUF provides the most consistent performance across models and bitwidths, and that language-matched calibration benefits low-bit quantization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Study:** The paper conducts a large-scale and systematic evaluation across a wide range of languages, model sizes, and quantization techniques. This breadth is a major strength, providing a more complete picture of the impact of PTQ on multilingual MT than previous works.
    *   **Focus on Low-Resource Languages:** By specifically examining the performance on low-resource languages, the paper addresses a critical gap in the literature, which often focuses primarily on English or high-resource languages. This highlights the potential for inequitable outcomes when deploying quantized LLMs.
    *   **Algorithm Comparison:** The comparison of different quantization algorithms is valuable, offering practical guidance for practitioners selecting quantization methods for their multilingual MT deployments. The finding that GGUF consistently performs well, even at very low bitwidths, is particularly useful.
    *   **Hyperparameter Analysis:** The investigation of decoding hyperparameters and calibration data contributes to a deeper understanding of the interactions between quantization and other aspects of MT inference.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The paper delivers clear, actionable insights for deploying multilingual LLMs, especially in low-resource settings, by outlining the effects of PTQ on translation quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Automatic Evaluation Metric (COMET):**  While COMET is a strong metric, the paper relies primarily on it for evaluation.  It is well known that absolute COMET scores can be misleading (as the authors themselves point out) and that even relative COMET scores are best compared within a single language pair.  A more robust evaluation would include a significant component of human evaluation to validate the trends observed via COMET, especially concerning low-resource language degradation.
    *   **Limited Ablation of Quantization Parameters:**  While different quantization *techniques* were compared, there was limited exploration of the *internal* hyperparameters of each (e.g., group sizes in GGUF beyond the given defaults, channel groupings within AutoAWQ beyond their defaults). Further ablation would strengthen the understanding.
    *   **Narrow Search Space of Decoding Parameters:** Although decoding parameters such as temperature and top-p are analyzed, the explored space is somewhat narrow and misses alternative sampling strategies.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   **Novelty:** While PTQ itself is not novel, the paper's systematic and comprehensive evaluation of PTQ across a large number of languages for MT is a significant contribution. The detailed comparison of quantization algorithms and the analysis of decoding hyperparameters specifically within the context of multilingual MT adds to the novelty.
    *   **Significance:** The paper is highly significant because it addresses the practical challenges of deploying LLMs for multilingual MT, particularly in resource-constrained settings. The findings can inform the development of more robust and equitable AI systems that are accessible to a wider range of languages. It highlights the risks of overlooking linguistic diversity when optimizing LLMs for deployment, pushing for more inclusive evaluation and optimization strategies.

**Justification:**

The paper has significant value in providing a systematic, well-executed study, particularly due to the focus on the under-explored area of low-resource languages and multilingual MT. The sheer scale and scope of the experiments, alongside the practical insights derived regarding specific quantization methods and calibration, justify a solid score.  However, the heavy reliance on COMET and the relatively limited parameter sweeps during training limit the strength of the overall findings and the confidence with which we can generalize from them.  For the score given below, COMET is weighted to be a strong argument, limiting the ceiling to 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Language-Enhanced Mobile Manipulation for Efficient Object Search in Indoor Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20899v1)
- **Authors**: Liding Zhang, Zeqi Li, Kuanqi Cai, Qian Huang, Zhenshan Bing, Alois Knoll
- **Abstract**: Enabling robots to efficiently search for and identify objects in complex, unstructured environments is critical for diverse applications ranging from household assistance to industrial automation. However, traditional scene representations typically capture only static semantics and lack interpretable contextual reasoning, limiting their ability to guide object search in completely unfamiliar settings. To address this challenge, we propose a language-enhanced hierarchical navigation framework that tightly integrates semantic perception and spatial reasoning. Our method, Goal-Oriented Dynamically Heuristic-Guided Hierarchical Search (GODHS), leverages large language models (LLMs) to infer scene semantics and guide the search process through a multi-level decision hierarchy. Reliability in reasoning is achieved through the use of structured prompts and logical constraints applied at each stage of the hierarchy. For the specific challenges of mobile manipulation, we introduce a heuristic-based motion planner that combines polar angle sorting with distance prioritization to efficiently generate exploration paths. Comprehensive evaluations in Isaac Sim demonstrate the feasibility of our framework, showing that GODHS can locate target objects with higher search efficiency compared to conventional, non-semantic search strategies. Website and Video are available at: https://drapandiger.github.io/GODHS
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a language-enhanced mobile manipulation framework called Goal-Oriented Dynamically Heuristic-Guided Hierarchical Search (GODHS) designed for efficient object search in indoor environments. GODHS uses a five-level hierarchy (Scene -> Room -> Carrier -> Feature -> Item) guided by Large Language Models (LLMs) to decompose the search task.  Structured prompts and logical constraints are used to ensure the reliability of the LLM reasoning. A heuristic-based motion planner using polar angle sorting and distance prioritization is proposed for efficient exploration within each carrier. The system's feasibility is demonstrated through simulations, showing improved search efficiency compared to non-semantic strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of LLM-driven hierarchical reasoning with a structured mobile manipulation framework.  While individual components (LLMs for planning, hierarchical search, heuristic motion planning) are not entirely new, their combination and the specific implementation, particularly the structured prompting approach, represent a significant contribution.  The paper effectively bridges the gap between high-level semantic reasoning and low-level robotic execution. Previous approaches often relied on either purely geometric heuristics or lacked the ability to effectively integrate commonsense knowledge for multi-step reasoning.
* **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial challenge in robotics: enabling robots to efficiently operate in complex, unstructured environments.  The GODHS framework demonstrates a promising approach to overcome the limitations of traditional purely spatial exploration methods.  By incorporating semantic knowledge, the robot can significantly reduce the search space and improve efficiency. The performance gains documented in the simulation environment are compelling and suggest the potential for real-world impact.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written and provides a clear explanation of the GODHS framework, including the LLM prompting strategy and the heuristic motion planner.
    * **Effective Integration of LLMs:** The use of structured prompts and logical constraints addresses the reliability issues associated with LLMs and ensures that their reasoning is grounded in the specific task requirements.
    * **Heuristic Motion Planner:** The motion planner offers a practical approach to exploring carriers efficiently, reducing redundant motion.
    * **Strong Experimental Evaluation:** The simulation results demonstrate the effectiveness of the GODHS framework compared to baseline methods. The controlled setting of Isaac Sim allows for systematic evaluations.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Simulation:** The evaluation is limited to a simulated environment. While Isaac Sim offers realistic physics, real-world complexities (sensor noise, dynamic environments, imperfect semantic segmentation) could significantly impact performance. Transferring the simulation setup to a physical robot experiment is crucial to validate this.
    * **LLM Dependency:** The system's performance heavily relies on the accuracy and common-sense knowledge of the LLM. Performance may degrade if the LLM encounters unfamiliar objects, relations, or hallucinate.
    * **Limited Failure Mode Analysis:**  While the paper identifies some failure modes, a more in-depth analysis would be beneficial. Quantifying the frequency and causes of failures will help guide future research directions.
    * **Scalability:** The paper does not discuss the scalability of the approach to larger and more complex environments. The LLM's inference time could become a bottleneck in such cases.

* **Potential Impact:** The GODHS framework has the potential to influence future research in mobile manipulation, particularly in areas such as household robotics, industrial automation, and search and rescue. The integration of LLMs with robotic systems is a rapidly growing field, and this work provides a valuable contribution by demonstrating a practical and effective approach.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the integrated framework, the significance of the addressed problem, the clear presentation, and the promising simulation results, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field. However, the heavy reliance on simulation and a more in-depth investigation of failure modes are important considerations. Therefore, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Research Challenges in Relational Database Management Systems for LLM Queries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20912v1)
- **Authors**: Kerem Akillioglu, Anurag Chakraborty, Sairaj Voruganti, M. Tamer Özsu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have become essential for applications such as text summarization, sentiment analysis, and automated question-answering. Recently, LLMs have also been integrated into relational database management systems to enhance querying and support advanced data processing. Companies such as Amazon, Databricks, Google, and Snowflake offer LLM invocation directly within SQL, denoted as LLM queries, to boost data insights. However, open-source solutions currently have limited functionality and poor performance. In this work, we present an early exploration of two open-source systems and one enterprise platform, using five representative queries to expose functional, performance, and scalability limits in today's SQL-invoked LLM integrations. We identify three main issues: enforcing structured outputs, optimizing resource utilization, and improving query planning. We implemented initial solutions and observed improvements in accommodating LLM powered SQL queries. These early gains demonstrate that tighter integration of LLM+DBMS is the key to scalable and efficient processing of LLM queries.
- **Summary**: This paper explores the challenges of integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into relational database management systems (DBMS) for enhanced query processing. It evaluates the functionality, performance, and scalability limitations of three systems: PostgreSQL with pgAI, DuckDB with FlockMTL (both open-source), and MotherDuck (an enterprise solution). The authors analyze five representative SQL queries that invoke LLMs and identify key issues: enforcing structured outputs, optimizing resource utilization, and improving query planning. They implement initial solutions and demonstrate improvements in accommodating LLM-powered SQL queries, highlighting the potential of tighter LLM+DBMS integration for scalable and efficient processing of AI-enhanced workloads.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and timely problem: the integration of LLMs into database systems. The increasing use of LLMs for data analytics and querying makes this a crucial area for research. The paper offers a preliminary yet valuable exploration of the functional, performance, and scalability hurdles involved.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Relevance:** The paper focuses on a practical problem that is of growing importance to database researchers and practitioners.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** The evaluation of existing systems (both open-source and enterprise) provides valuable insights into the current state of LLM+DBMS integration.
*   **Clear Problem Identification:** The paper clearly identifies the key challenges (structured outputs, resource utilization, and query planning) and provides concrete examples of how these challenges manifest in real-world scenarios.
*   **Initial Solutions:** While not fully comprehensive, the authors' initial solutions demonstrate the potential for improvement through tighter LLM+DBMS integration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to a specific set of queries and a relatively small dataset size. The generalizability of the findings to other types of LLM queries and larger datasets may be limited.
*   **Shallow Analysis:** While the paper identifies the challenges, the analysis of the underlying causes and potential solutions could be more in-depth. The solutions implemented are initial and somewhat ad-hoc, lacking a comprehensive theoretical framework.
*   **Lack of Novelty:** The paper primarily focuses on identifying and characterizing existing problems. While the initial solutions are useful, they don't represent significant novel contributions to the field. Many of the mentioned challenges and solutions (e.g., constrained decoding, batching) are known in the broader LLM and DBMS communities. The novelty lies in specifically applying and analyzing them in the LLM+DBMS context.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its early exploration of a challenging and important problem. It serves as a useful starting point for further research by highlighting the key issues that need to be addressed to enable scalable and efficient LLM-powered SQL queries. The paper can motivate further research on optimization techniques, query planning algorithms, and resource management strategies specifically tailored for LLM+DBMS integration.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field by highlighting practical challenges in LLM+DBMS integration and exploring initial solutions. However, its limited scope, shallow analysis, and lack of significant novelty prevent it from achieving a higher score. The paper's primary contribution is in identifying and characterizing existing problems in the specific context of LLM-enhanced relational databases, rather than introducing completely new theoretical or algorithmic breakthroughs. While the experimental results demonstrate some improvements, they also reveal that significant challenges remain to achieve truly scalable and efficient processing of LLM queries.
Therefore, given the paper's strengths (practical relevance, empirical evaluation) and weaknesses (limited scope, shallow analysis, limited novelty), a score of 6 is appropriate.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 6/10

### SageLM: A Multi-aspect and Explainable Large Language Model for Speech Judgement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20916v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan Ge, Junxiang Zhang, Xiaoqian Liu, Bei Li, Xiangnan Ma, Chenglong Wang, Kaiyang Ye, Yangfan Du, Linfeng Zhang, Yuxin Huang, Tong Xiao, Zhengtao Yu, JingBo Zhu
- **Abstract**: Speech-to-Speech (S2S) Large Language Models (LLMs) are foundational to natural human-computer interaction, enabling end-to-end spoken dialogue systems. However, evaluating these models remains a fundamental challenge. We propose \texttt{SageLM}, an end-to-end, multi-aspect, and explainable speech LLM for comprehensive S2S LLMs evaluation. First, unlike cascaded approaches that disregard acoustic features, SageLM jointly assesses both semantic and acoustic dimensions. Second, it leverages rationale-based supervision to enhance explainability and guide model learning, achieving superior alignment with evaluation outcomes compared to rule-based reinforcement learning methods. Third, we introduce \textit{SpeechFeedback}, a synthetic preference dataset, and employ a two-stage training paradigm to mitigate the scarcity of speech preference data. Trained on both semantic and acoustic dimensions, SageLM achieves an 82.79\% agreement rate with human evaluators, outperforming cascaded and SLM-based baselines by at least 7.42\% and 26.20\%, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SageLMS: A Multi-aspect and Explainable Large Language Model for Speech Judgement":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SageLM, an end-to-end multi-aspect, and explainable judge large language model (LLM) specifically designed for evaluating speech-to-speech (S2S) LLMs.  SageLM addresses limitations of existing evaluation methods, particularly cascaded ASR-then-text-LLM pipelines, which ignore acoustic features and suffer from error propagation, and expensive human evaluations. SageLM assesses both semantic and acoustic dimensions, leverages rationale-based supervision for explainability, and uses a two-stage training paradigm to mitigate data scarcity. The paper demonstrates SageLM achieves state-of-the-art performance in agreement with human evaluators. They create a new speech preference dataset called SpeechFeedback.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates moderate novelty. The concept of using LLMs as judges is established, however, directly judging speech and incorporating acoustic aspects within that framework provides incremental novelty. The creation of SpeechFeedback, a comprehensive synthetic speech preference dataset, is a valuable contribution, directly addressing the data scarcity problem.  The two-stage training approach using rationale-augmented SFT is another positive aspect.

*   **Significance:** The paper holds medium-high significance.  Accurate and scalable evaluation of S2S LLMs is a bottleneck in the field. SageLM offers a promising alternative to existing methods, particularly by incorporating acoustic assessment and providing explainable judgments. Improved speech dialogue evaluation could unlock faster and cheaper progress in S2S modeling. The release of SpeechFeedback will serve as a valuable resource for the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a clear and important problem: the evaluation of S2S LLMs.
    *   Proposes a holistic approach, considering both semantic and acoustic aspects.
    *   Introduces a new, large-scale dataset for speech preference learning.
    *   Demonstrates strong experimental results, outperforming baselines.
    *   Emphasizes explainability through rationale-based supervision.
    *   Addresses the data scarcity problem with a sensible two-stage training strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The synthetic nature of SpeechFeedback introduces a potential domain gap and may not fully capture the complexities of real-world human speech interactions. This limitation is acknowledged in the paper, where they use textual queries as input to ensure a fair comparison across S2S models. Further experiments and validation using real human speech data are needed.
    *   While the paper uses human evaluation for testing, the training relies on synthetically generated preferences and rationales, potentially biasing the model.
    *   The paper doesn't deeply investigate or benchmark against other possible approaches (e.g., fine-tuning speech encoders and using them as embeddings). The experimentation is also limited to models in the Qwen and Whisper families.
    *   The limitations of the 60s limit are noted, however, the paper does not propose or explore alternative training options to alleviate such issues.

*   **Potential Impact:**  SageLM has the potential to influence the field by providing a more comprehensive, scalable, and explainable solution for evaluating S2S LLMs. SpeechFeedback could become a widely used benchmark dataset. The paper provides a new baseline for speech evaluation.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*   The novelty is present with the end-to-end approach and the explicit incorporation of acoustic features and explainability.
*   The significance is medium-high, given the importance of S2S evaluation. The creation of SpeechFeedback dataset significantly address a key problem.
*   The paper has several strengths in terms of methodology, experimental results, and clarity of presentation.
*   However, the reliance on synthetic data for training and the need for more extensive validation with real human data, along with potential limitations in the experimental setup and the discussion of alternative approaches, limit the score.
*   The paper makes a good and needed contribution but it’s an incremental, rather than a revolutionary, one. The synthetic data, though comprehensive, might not entirely translate to real-world scenarios.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### How Can Input Reformulation Improve Tool Usage Accuracy in a Complex Dynamic Environment? A Study on $τ$-bench
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20931v1)
- **Authors**: Venkatesh Mishra, Amir Saeidi, Satyam Raj, Mutsumi Nakamura, Jayanth Srinivasa, Gaowen Liu, Ali Payani, Chitta Baral
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in reasoning and planning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) have enabled their potential as autonomous agents capable of tool use in dynamic environments. However, in multi-turn conversational environments like $\tau$-bench, these agents often struggle with consistent reasoning, adherence to domain-specific policies, and extracting correct information over a long horizon of tool-calls and conversation. To capture and mitigate these failures, we conduct a comprehensive manual analysis of the common errors occurring in the conversation trajectories. We then experiment with reformulations of inputs to the tool-calling agent for improvement in agent decision making. Finally, we propose the Input-Reformulation Multi-Agent (IRMA) framework, which automatically reformulates user queries augmented with relevant domain rules and tool suggestions for the tool-calling agent to focus on. The results show that IRMA significantly outperforms ReAct, Function Calling, and Self-Reflection by 16.1%, 12.7%, and 19.1%, respectively, in overall pass^5 scores. These findings highlight the superior reliability and consistency of IRMA compared to other methods in dynamic environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of using Large Language Models (LLMs) as autonomous agents for tool usage in complex, dynamic environments like the T-bench benchmark. It identifies common failure modes such as user instruction hallucination, agent hallucination, domain policy violations, and contextual misinterpretation.  The authors propose the Input-Reformulation Multi-Agent (IRMA) framework, which reformulates user queries by augmenting them with relevant domain rules and tool suggestions to improve agent decision-making. IRMA leverages modules for memorization, constraint management, and tool suggestion. Experimental results show that IRMA outperforms ReAct, Function Calling, and Self-Reflection in terms of accuracy, reliability, and consistency, as measured by pass@k metrics on the T-bench dataset.  It also reduces the number of interaction turns required and exhibits robustness to errors in the ground truth data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the IRMA framework's approach to improving tool usage by focusing on input reformulation rather than post-hoc action correction. While context augmentation is not entirely new, the specific combination of memorization, constraint integration, and tool suggestion within a multi-agent framework and its application to the tool-calling problem is a valuable contribution. The detailed error classification and its use to guide the design of IRMA also add to the novelty.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical contribution to making LLM-based agents more reliable and consistent in complex, real-world scenarios. The empirical results convincingly demonstrate IRMA's superior performance on the T-bench benchmark compared to existing methods. The findings have direct implications for developing more robust and trustworthy agents for tasks such as travel booking, customer support, and enterprise operations.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of the failure modes of LLM-based agents in tool-calling scenarios.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** IRMA is a clearly articulated and well-engineered framework with distinct modules.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results that demonstrate the effectiveness of IRMA on T-bench. The ablation studies provide further insights into the importance of each module.
*   **Focus on Reliability and Consistency:** The paper emphasizes the importance of reliability and consistency (measured by pass@k) in real-world applications, which is often overlooked in other studies that focus solely on accuracy (pass@1).
*   **Ablation Studies:** The results of the ablation studies allow readers to understand the impact of each component of the IRMA framework on overall performance, contributing to a deeper insight into the framework.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Benchmark Dependence:** The evaluation is primarily conducted on the T-bench benchmark, which limits the generalizability of the results. Testing IRMA on other datasets would strengthen the claim of its robustness. Although T-bench emulates realistic scenarios, there is a potential that biases specific to that benchmark may have influenced the observed performance gains.
*   **Scope of Automation:**  While IRMA automates input reformulation, the error classification and design of the specific modules might require significant human effort and domain expertise to adapt to new tasks. A more automated or generalizable method for determining the relevant constraints and tool suggestions would be a valuable extension.
*   **Scalability and Computational Cost:** The paper does not explicitly address the scalability and computational cost of the IRMA framework, particularly the overhead of the multi-agent system involved in input reformulation.
*   **User Instruction Hallucination:** The paper mentions "User Instruction Hallucination". While the other error categories such as Domain Policy Violation are clear and defined, "User Instruction Hallucination" is more vague in its description.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM-based agents by addressing the challenges of tool usage in complex, dynamic environments.  The IRMA framework and the thorough analysis of failure modes provide a practical and insightful approach to improving agent reliability and consistency. However, the dependence on a single benchmark, the limited scope of automation, and the lack of scalability analysis prevent the paper from being a truly exceptional contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DrivingGaussian++: Towards Realistic Reconstruction and Editable Simulation for Surrounding Dynamic Driving Scenes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20965v1)
- **Authors**: Yajiao Xiong, Xiaoyu Zhou, Yongtao Wan, Deqing Sun, Ming-Hsuan Yang
- **Abstract**: We present DrivingGaussian++, an efficient and effective framework for realistic reconstructing and controllable editing of surrounding dynamic autonomous driving scenes. DrivingGaussian++ models the static background using incremental 3D Gaussians and reconstructs moving objects with a composite dynamic Gaussian graph, ensuring accurate positions and occlusions. By integrating a LiDAR prior, it achieves detailed and consistent scene reconstruction, outperforming existing methods in dynamic scene reconstruction and photorealistic surround-view synthesis. DrivingGaussian++ supports training-free controllable editing for dynamic driving scenes, including texture modification, weather simulation, and object manipulation, leveraging multi-view images and depth priors. By integrating large language models (LLMs) and controllable editing, our method can automatically generate dynamic object motion trajectories and enhance their realism during the optimization process. DrivingGaussian++ demonstrates consistent and realistic editing results and generates dynamic multi-view driving scenarios, while significantly enhancing scene diversity. More results and code can be found at the project site: https://xiong-creator.github.io/DrivingGaussian_plus.github.io
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DrivingGaussian++ paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DrivingGaussian++, a framework for reconstructing and editing dynamic autonomous driving scenes. It combines incremental 3D Gaussians for the static background with a composite dynamic Gaussian graph for moving objects. This hierarchical approach aims to achieve realistic reconstruction, handle occlusions, and facilitate controllable editing. Key aspects include incorporating a LiDAR prior for enhanced geometry and multi-view consistency, supporting training-free editing (texture modification, weather simulation, object manipulation), and leveraging large language models (LLMs) to generate realistic dynamic object motion trajectories. The framework significantly improves scene diversity and provides consistent and realistic editing results, all while being efficient in terms of computational cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **Strengths:** The paper introduces a composite Gaussian Splatting approach that decomposes the scene into static and dynamic components, offering a hierarchical representation tailored to driving environments. The integration of LiDAR data for initialization is a smart move that enhances geometric accuracy. The training-free editing paradigm and the LLM-driven motion prediction are notable innovations, making the system more practical and versatile.

    *   **Weaknesses:**  While the combination of elements is novel, many individual components (Gaussian Splatting, LiDAR integration, diffusion-based editing, LLMs for motion) are built upon existing research. The system is more of an *integration* of existing techniques than a radical departure in any single area.  The reliance on existing models like GroundingDINO and diffusion models means that the performance is inherently limited by these pre-trained models.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Strengths:**  The ability to realistically reconstruct and edit driving scenes has significant implications for autonomous driving simulation and data augmentation. The training-free editing paradigm makes the system much more accessible than methods requiring retraining for each modification. The improvements in multi-view consistency are particularly important in multi-camera driving scenarios. The use of LLMs for motion prediction brings a new level of realism and complexity to dynamic scene generation, going beyond simple pre-defined trajectories. DrivingGaussian++ shows superior performance in qualitative and quantitative comparisons, suggesting it offers a valuable advance in the field.
    *   **Weaknesses:** The results might be limited by the nature of the training datasets, that the performance gains might be somewhat restricted to driving scene scenarios, and that its general applicability to other dynamic 3D scene editing might be limited. The editing framework, while training-free, still relies on a sequence of steps and decisions made by the user, rather than a more automated or end-to-end approach.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper provides a valuable contribution by integrating existing techniques into a coherent and efficient framework specifically designed for dynamic driving scene editing. The novel aspects, such as the composite Gaussian splatting and LLM integration for motion prediction, enhance the system's capabilities and offer a tangible improvement over existing methods. While not revolutionary, the significance of its ability to realistically reconstruct and edit driving scenes with LiDAR and its integration of an LLM is considerable and should contribute to more realistic and improved autonomous driving systems.
     *   The main weaknesses being the reliance on existing pre-trained models and its constrained applicability to driving scenes.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ProactiveEval: A Unified Evaluation Framework for Proactive Dialogue Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20973v1)
- **Authors**: Tianjian Liu, Fanqi Wan, Jiajian Guo, Xiaojun Quan
- **Abstract**: Proactive dialogue has emerged as a critical and challenging research problem in advancing large language models (LLMs). Existing works predominantly focus on domain-specific or task-oriented scenarios, which leads to fragmented evaluations and limits the comprehensive exploration of models' proactive conversation abilities. In this work, we propose ProactiveEval, a unified framework designed for evaluating proactive dialogue capabilities of LLMs. This framework decomposes proactive dialogue into target planning and dialogue guidance, establishing evaluation metrics across various domains. Moreover, it also enables the automatic generation of diverse and challenging evaluation data. Based on the proposed framework, we develop 328 evaluation environments spanning 6 distinct domains. Through experiments with 22 different types of LLMs, we show that DeepSeek-R1 and Claude-3.7-Sonnet exhibit exceptional performance on target planning and dialogue guidance tasks, respectively. Finally, we investigate how reasoning capabilities influence proactive behaviors and discuss their implications for future model development.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ProactiveEval: A Unified Evaluation Framework for Proactive Dialogue Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ProactiveEval, a unified framework designed to evaluate the proactive dialogue capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs).  The framework decomposes proactive dialogue into two key tasks: target planning and dialogue guidance. It leverages "LLM-as-a-judge" with task-specific evaluation dimensions and proposes an evaluation data synthesis framework for automatically generating diverse and challenging data. They create 328 evaluation environments across six domains (one without a public benchmark) and assess 22 LLMs, identifying DeepSeek-R1 and Claude-3.7-Sonnet as top performers in target planning and dialogue guidance, respectively. The study also explores the influence of reasoning capabilities on proactive behaviors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive and unified approach to evaluating proactive dialogue agents. While individual components (LLM-as-a-judge, data synthesis techniques) have been explored in isolation, their integration into a coherent framework specifically tailored for proactive dialogue is a significant contribution. The creation of evaluation environments spanning several domains, particularly the Glasses Assistant domain which lacked previous benchmarks, adds value. However, certain aspects, such as the data synthesis techniques and LLM-as-a-judge, are adapted from prior work, which moderately lowers the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the evaluation of LLMs, particularly in their ability to engage in proactive conversations. The lack of standardized benchmarks has made it difficult to compare different models and assess progress. By providing a unified framework, ProactiveEval enables more systematic and comprehensive evaluations, which could drive future research in proactive dialogue agents. The analysis of reasoning capabilities' influence on proactive behaviors offers insights into current limitations and future directions for model development.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:**  The division into target planning and dialogue guidance provides a structured approach to evaluating proactivity.
    *   **Automated Data Synthesis:** The ability to automatically generate challenging evaluation data is a significant advantage.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:**  The evaluation of 22 LLMs across 328 environments provides a solid empirical basis for the findings.
    *   **Inclusion of a Domain Lacking Benchmarks:**  Addressing Glasses Assistant proactivity provides resources for a specific niche.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis on the influence of reasoning provides valuable insights for model development.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge:** While convenient, LLM-as-a-judge inherently introduces potential biases. The level of consistency with human judgement is high, but there are still concerns.
    *   **Simulated User Interaction:** The reliance on simulated users in dialogue guidance may not fully capture the complexities of real-world human-agent interactions.  The agreeableness parameter offers a limited representation of user behavior.
    *   **Limited Investigation of Reasoning Capabilities:** While the paper investigates the impact of "thinking behavior", the specific reasoning mechanisms and their interplay with proactivity could be explored in greater depth. The improvement of dialogue guidance capabilities from thinking appears minimal.

*   **Potential Influence:** ProactiveEval has the potential to become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working on proactive dialogue agents. It provides a standardized way to evaluate models, compare different approaches, and identify areas for improvement. The generated dataset could serve as a benchmark for future research. However, to achieve widespread adoption, the framework needs to be readily accessible, well-documented, and regularly updated to reflect the evolving capabilities of LLMs.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of proactive dialogue by providing a much-needed unified evaluation framework. While certain aspects rely on existing techniques and the simulated nature of the evaluations has limitations, the comprehensiveness, automated data generation, and extensive empirical analysis of ProactiveEval warrant a relatively high score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Neuro-Symbolic Learning of Constraints and Objective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20978v1)
- **Authors**: Marianne Defresne, Romain Gambardella, Sophie Barbe, Thomas Schiex
- **Abstract**: In the ongoing quest for hybridizing discrete reasoning with neural nets, there is an increasing interest in neural architectures that can learn how to solve discrete reasoning or optimization problems from natural inputs, a task that Large Language Models seem to struggle with. Objectives: We introduce a differentiable neuro-symbolic architecture and a loss function dedicated to learning how to solve NP-hard reasoning problems. Methods: Our new probabilistic loss allows for learning both the constraints and the objective, thus delivering a complete model that can be scrutinized and completed with side constraints. By pushing the combinatorial solver out of the training loop, our architecture also offers scalable training while exact inference gives access to maximum accuracy. Results: We empirically show that it can efficiently learn how to solve NP-hard reasoning problems from natural inputs. On three variants of the Sudoku benchmark -- symbolic, visual, and many-solution --, our approach requires a fraction of training time of other hybrid methods. On a visual Min-Cut/Max-cut task, it optimizes the regret better than a Decision-Focused-Learning regret-dedicated loss. Finally, it efficiently learns the energy optimization formulation of the large real-world problem of designing proteins.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel neuro-symbolic architecture and loss function (E-PLL) for learning how to solve NP-hard reasoning problems from natural inputs. The architecture combines deep learning layers with a final discrete graphical model (GM) reasoning layer. The E-PLL loss function addresses the limitations of the Negative Pseudo-LogLikelihood (NPLL) in handling constraints and large costs.  The approach allows for scalable training, exact inference for maximum accuracy, and the ability to scrutinize and complete the learned model with side constraints. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach on Sudoku variations (symbolic, visual, many-solution), visual Min-Cut/Max-Cut, and protein design problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the introduction of the E-PLL loss function and its integration into a neuro-symbolic architecture for solving NP-hard problems. While neuro-symbolic approaches are not entirely new, the specific combination of a differentiable loss tailored for constraint learning within this architecture is a significant contribution. The explicit focus on constraint learning and addressing the limitations of the NPLL distinguishes this work from general neuro-symbolic methods. The integration of the l1 regularization together with the e-pll is novel as well.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is multi-faceted:

    *   **Improved constraint learning:** The E-PLL demonstrably addresses a known weakness of NPLL in constraint-heavy problems.
    *   **Scalability:** The architecture decouples the combinatorial solver from the training loop, enabling scalable training on large datasets.
    *   **Interpretability:** The use of GMs provides a more interpretable model compared to black-box neural networks, which is crucial for trust and verification.
    *   **Real-world application:**  The application to protein design highlights the potential of the architecture to tackle complex, real-world problems.
    *   **Improved performance:** The results on several benchmarks show improvement in performance over existing hybrid models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a new architecture and loss function.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The authors provide a detailed description of the architecture, loss function, and experimental setup.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The approach is extensively evaluated on several benchmarks, demonstrating its effectiveness across different problem domains.
    *   **Theoretical grounding:** The analysis of the NPLL and the justification for the E-PLL are well-presented.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** While the E-PLL addresses the limitations of the NPLL, the masking component adds complexity, even though the paper demonstrates it works well.
    *   **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** The k parameter of the E-PLL seems not to be so important, as it is stated in the paper. A deeper analysis of how parameter λ affects the E-PLL performance could further strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited comparison:** While the paper compares against relevant baselines, direct comparisons to other state-of-the-art neuro-symbolic methods for specific tasks could further emphasize the benefits of the proposed approach.
    *   **Scalability to extremely large instances:** Although the paper demonstrates scalability, it would be helpful to see results on even larger protein design instances or more complex, real-world constraint satisfaction problems.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of neuro-symbolic reasoning. The E-PLL loss function could become a standard tool for learning constraints in hybrid architectures. The scalability of the approach opens up possibilities for tackling larger and more complex problems.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of neuro-symbolic reasoning. The E-PLL loss function effectively addresses the limitations of the NPLL in constraint learning, leading to improved performance on various benchmarks. The scalability and interpretability of the architecture are also valuable strengths. While there is room for further improvement in the analysis of the hyperparameters or comparison against other hybrid models, the paper is well-motivated, rigorously evaluated, and has the potential to influence future research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### ChatThero: An LLM-Supported Chatbot for Behavior Change and Therapeutic Support in Addiction Recovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20996v1)
- **Authors**: Junda Wang, Zonghai Yao, Zhichao Yang, Lingxi Li, Junhui Qian, Hong Yu
- **Abstract**: Substance use disorders (SUDs) affect over 36 million people worldwide, yet few receive effective care due to stigma, motivational barriers, and limited personalized support. Although large language models (LLMs) show promise for mental-health assistance, most systems lack tight integration with clinically validated strategies, reducing effectiveness in addiction recovery. We present ChatThero, a multi-agent conversational framework that couples dynamic patient modeling with context-sensitive therapeutic dialogue and adaptive persuasive strategies grounded in cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT) and motivational interviewing (MI). We build a high-fidelity synthetic benchmark spanning Easy, Medium, and Hard resistance levels, and train ChatThero with a two-stage pipeline comprising supervised fine-tuning (SFT) followed by direct preference optimization (DPO). In evaluation, ChatThero yields a 41.5\% average gain in patient motivation, a 0.49\% increase in treatment confidence, and resolves hard cases with 26\% fewer turns than GPT-4o, and both automated and human clinical assessments rate it higher in empathy, responsiveness, and behavioral realism. The framework supports rigorous, privacy-preserving study of therapeutic conversation and provides a robust, replicable basis for research and clinical translation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous critical evaluation of the paper "ChatThero: An LLM-Supported Chatbot for Behavior Change and Therapeutic Support in Addiction Recovery":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ChatThero, a novel AI-driven conversational framework designed to support addiction recovery.  Unlike general-purpose chatbots, ChatThero integrates dynamically evolving patient profiles, adaptive memory, and multi-agent interactions grounded in clinically validated strategies like Cognitive Behavioral Therapy (CBT) and Motivational Interviewing (MI). The system is trained using a two-stage pipeline: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with synthetic therapeutic dialogues, followed by direct preference optimization (DPO) guided by expert and AI feedback.  The framework demonstrates superior performance compared to advanced LLM baselines in simulated dialogues, particularly in challenging scenarios where patients exhibit resistance. Human evaluators also confirm ChatThero's superior empathetic responsiveness and clinical relevance, emphasizing its potential for sustained therapeutic engagement in realistic addiction recovery interactions.  The authors address ethical concerns through data anonymization, synthesized profiles, and controlled simulations, positioning the study as exploratory. They make their code and dataset construction scripts available for reproducibility and further research.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles the crucial and under-addressed issue of providing scalable and personalized support for addiction recovery. The high relapse rates and treatment gaps highlight the need for innovative solutions.
    *   **Novel Framework:** ChatThero's integration of dynamic patient profiles, adaptive memory, and multi-agent interactions based on clinically validated techniques represents a novel approach. The careful combination of these elements makes it a promising research platform.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The two-stage training pipeline (SFT + DPO) seems well-suited for the task, allowing for initial learning from clinical dialogues and subsequent refinement based on expert and AI feedback. The use of synthetic data is justified by the sensitivity of real-world addiction narratives, but is also a current limitation.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The authors explicitly address ethical concerns by employing fully anonymized patient profiles and simulation-based evaluations, demonstrating responsible research practices.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study includes both quantitative metrics (motivation, confidence) and qualitative assessments from human evaluators, providing a well-rounded evaluation of ChatThero's effectiveness.
    *   **Reproducibility:** By releasing the code, data construction scripts, and synthetic data examples, the authors promote reproducibility and facilitate further research in the field.
    *   **Strong Performance:** The quantitative metrics for motivation and confidence gains show a clear improvement for ChatThero over GPT-4 in challenging settings. The human evaluation provides further backing to the AI driven data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Limitations:** The reliance on synthetic data, while ethically justifiable, limits the generalizability of the results to real-world clinical settings. Patient profiles and interactions, even when carefully simulated, may not fully capture the complexity and nuance of actual addiction recovery.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation primarily focuses on short-term conversational outcomes (empathy, responsiveness, strategic appropriateness). Longitudinal effects (patient trust, therapeutic alliance, sustained behavioral change) are not assessed.
    *   **Western-Centric Focus:** The study is limited to English-language, Western-context scenarios, raising concerns about the generalizability of the persuasive strategies to diverse cultural and linguistic backgrounds.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Baselines on Specific Tasks:** GPT-4 is a powerful baseline, but there may be other more specialized models in the area of mental health chatbots, or in LLM-based multi-agent framework evaluation, that could have given a better comparative understanding of ChatThero's relative strengths.
    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge:** While using an LLM as a judge is common, the potential biases and limitations of this approach should be acknowledged and addressed. Comparing with actual human-to-human interaction, and not simply human ratings, may be an important next step to validate the clinical viability of the methodology.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    The paper introduces a well-structured framework that combines a series of novel techniques for an important problem space.

    *   The use of a multi-agent architecture in this domain is fairly novel, and helps to address the complex and high-dimensional interactions between patient, environment and therapist.
    *   The ethical-centric simulation approach allows for the validation of novel training and evaluation methodologies.
    *   The ability of ChatThero to maintain performance over difficult patient settings suggests an interesting area of innovation to pursue.

* **Potential Influence:** The proposed framework provides a robust, replicable basis for research and clinical translation in addiction recovery. The ability to simulate challenging patients, while maintaining empathy and compassion, allows researchers to pursue improved methodologies for AI-driven therapy. Further refinement and scaling of the ChatThero architecture may contribute to better outcomes in a range of patients, improving access and affordability for critical treatment areas.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the novel framework, the rigorous methodology, the ethical considerations, and the promising initial results, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field of AI-driven mental healthcare. However, the limitations related to synthetic data, limited scope of evaluation, and Western-centric focus prevent it from achieving a higher score. A more realistic validation with human subjects and real therapists, as well as a more comprehensive longitudinal study, would significantly improve the work's impact.

Score: 7

The score is a reflection of a well-executed paper that has the potential to initiate significant advancements in the field, but which requires considerable additional validation to achieve full impact in real-world clinical practice.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Lethe: Purifying Backdoored Large Language Models with Knowledge Dilution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21004v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Chen, Yuchen Sun, Jiaxin Gao, Xueluan Gong, Qian Wang, Ziyao Wang, Yongsen Zheng, Kwok-Yan Lam
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have seen significant advancements, achieving superior performance in various Natural Language Processing (NLP) tasks. However, they remain vulnerable to backdoor attacks, where models behave normally for standard queries but generate harmful responses or unintended output when specific triggers are activated. Existing backdoor defenses either lack comprehensiveness, focusing on narrow trigger settings, detection-only mechanisms, and limited domains, or fail to withstand advanced scenarios like model-editing-based, multi-trigger, and triggerless attacks. In this paper, we present LETHE, a novel method to eliminate backdoor behaviors from LLMs through knowledge dilution using both internal and external mechanisms. Internally, LETHE leverages a lightweight dataset to train a clean model, which is then merged with the backdoored model to neutralize malicious behaviors by diluting the backdoor impact within the model's parametric memory. Externally, LETHE incorporates benign and semantically relevant evidence into the prompt to distract LLM's attention from backdoor features. Experimental results on classification and generation domains across 5 widely used LLMs demonstrate that LETHE outperforms 8 state-of-the-art defense baselines against 8 backdoor attacks. LETHE reduces the attack success rate of advanced backdoor attacks by up to 98% while maintaining model utility. Furthermore, LETHE has proven to be cost-efficient and robust against adaptive backdoor attacks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Lethe: Purifying Backdoored Large Language Models with Knowledge Dilution":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LETHE, a novel method for removing backdoors from large language models (LLMs).  LETHE works by diluting the influence of backdoor triggers through knowledge dilution, using both internal (parameter-level) and external (input-level) mechanisms.  Internally, it trains a clean model on a small dataset and merges it with the backdoored model.  Externally, it incorporates benign and semantically relevant evidence (explanations of keywords in the input query) into the prompt to distract the LLM from backdoor features. The method is evaluated across classification and generation tasks on several popular LLMs, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing defense baselines against various backdoor attacks, including advanced ones like model editing, multi-trigger, and triggerless attacks. The paper also reports on LETHE's cost-efficiency and robustness against adaptive attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core concept of "knowledge dilution" is somewhat novel in the context of backdoor defense for LLMs. While individual components (model merging, prompt engineering with knowledge injection) have been explored before, the combined, systematic approach is new and the results indicate a good method. The integration of both parameter-level and prompt-level interventions is a valuable contribution. The paper is good in the way that it is agnostic to trigger and is general across domains which is a problem that a lot of papers face.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical and growing problem: the vulnerability of LLMs to backdoor attacks.  The demonstrated effectiveness of LETHE, especially against advanced attack scenarios where other defenses fail, suggests its potential for real-world application.  The approach tackles the problem of LLMs being used for nefarious tasks and potentially could be used as a deterrent. The framework has a lot of practicality and is useful for individual developers or small organizations that cannot afford local infrastructure.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Defense:** The combination of internal and external dilution provides a more holistic defense compared to methods relying solely on one approach.
    *   **Effectiveness:**  The experimental results clearly show that LETHE outperforms several state-of-the-art baselines, especially against sophisticated attacks.
    *   **Robustness:**  The tests against adaptive attacks and non-backdoored models indicate LETHE is relatively robust and doesn't significantly degrade performance when not needed.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The use of LoRA for training the clean model and the lightweight external dilution makes LETHE a computationally practical solution.
    *   **Clear and well-presented**: The paper is well-written and clearly presents the approach, experiments, and results. The ablation studies provide a good understanding of the contribution of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on WordNet:** The external dilution relies on WordNet for evidence retrieval. While WordNet is useful, relying on it might limit the type of evidence you can retrieve, potentially missing contextually relevant info not contained within the lexicon. You potentially could change the knowledge base or add your own.
    *   **Limited exploration of model merging techniques:** The paper justifies the selection of SLERP as a default, and results from experimentation of model merging methods are very similar.

*   **Potential Influence:**  LETHE has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   **Inspiring new defense mechanisms:** The knowledge dilution framework can serve as a foundation for future research in backdoor defense.
    *   **Providing a practical solution:**  LETHE can be adopted by LLM developers and users to mitigate backdoor threats.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 8. The paper presents a solid and relatively novel approach to backdoor defense for LLMs, backed by strong experimental results. LETHE addresses a significant problem and offers practical advantages in terms of efficiency and robustness. It is well written, with a few potential weaknesses concerning external knowledge and model merging. Overall, the contribution is above average to the field with potential for real world implications.

### ChainReaction! Structured Approach with Causal Chains as Intermediate Representations for Improved and Explainable Causal Video Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21010v1)
- **Authors**: Paritosh Parmar, Eric Peh, Basura Fernando
- **Abstract**: Existing Causal-Why Video Question Answering (VideoQA) models often struggle with higher-order reasoning, relying on opaque, monolithic pipelines that entangle video understanding, causal inference, and answer generation. These black-box approaches offer limited interpretability and tend to depend on shallow heuristics. We propose a novel, modular framework that explicitly decouples causal reasoning from answer generation, introducing natural language causal chains as interpretable intermediate representations. Inspired by human cognitive models, these structured cause-effect sequences bridge low-level video content with high-level causal reasoning, enabling transparent and logically coherent inference. Our two-stage architecture comprises a Causal Chain Extractor (CCE) that generates causal chains from video-question pairs, and a Causal Chain-Driven Answerer (CCDA) that produces answers grounded in these chains. To address the lack of annotated reasoning traces, we introduce a scalable method for generating high-quality causal chains from existing datasets using large language models. We also propose CauCo, a new evaluation metric for causality-oriented captioning. Experiments on three large-scale benchmarks demonstrate that our approach not only outperforms state-of-the-art models, but also yields substantial gains in explainability, user trust, and generalization -- positioning the CCE as a reusable causal reasoning engine across diverse domains. Project page: https://paritoshparmar.github.io/chainreaction/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new framework for Causal-Why Video Question Answering (VideoQA) that utilizes natural language causal chains as intermediate representations.  Instead of monolithic models, the approach decouples causal reasoning from answer generation using two modules: a Causal Chain Extractor (CCE) and a Causal Chain-Driven Answerer (CCDA).  The CCE generates causal chains from videos based on questions, and the CCDA uses these chains to answer questions.  The authors address the lack of causal chain annotations by developing a method to generate these chains from existing VideoQA datasets using large language models. They also introduce a new evaluation metric, CauCo, for causality-oriented captioning.  Experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate improved performance, explainability, and generalization compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in explicitly using natural language causal chains as *interpretable* intermediate representations in a modular VideoQA framework.  While other works have explored causality in video understanding or used chain-of-thought prompting, this paper combines these ideas in a structured and explicit manner, focusing on *causal explanations* rather than just event recognition or description. The proposed approach directly integrates SCMs, and Chain-of-Thought reasoning in a robust & interpretable way. Also, the CauCo metric appears to be a novel contribution addressing a gap in evaluating causality-oriented captions. The efficient method for generating causal chain annotations from existing datasets is another significant innovation.

*   **Significance:**  The significance is multifaceted:

    *   **Improved Performance:** The paper reports superior performance compared to SOTA models on multiple benchmarks, which establishes the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Enhanced Explainability:** The human studies provide evidence that causal chains improve explainability, user trust, and debugging capabilities. This is a crucial step towards building more transparent and reliable AI systems. The use of causal chains offers a pathway to understand *why* a model makes certain predictions, rather than treating it as a black box.
    *   **Generalization:**  Demonstrating that the CCE generalizes to out-of-domain datasets is important, suggesting that it can serve as a reusable reasoning engine across different domains.
    *   **Methodological Contribution:** The causal chain generation and CauCo metrics serve as valuable resources for the community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Principled Approach:**  The use of SCMs provides a solid theoretical foundation for the work.
    *   **Modular Design:**  The two-stage architecture enables focused processing and easier debugging.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes quantitative results, human studies, and qualitative examples.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The causal chain generation method makes it possible to apply the approach to existing datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The causal chain generation depends on the capabilities of large language models. While the verification process is rigorous, the quality of the generated chains is still limited by the LLM's knowledge and reasoning abilities. It is important to note that they leverage, but not train the LLMs to generate causal chains.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis:**  While there are qualitative examples of failure cases, a more detailed error analysis could provide insights into the limitations of the approach and guide future work.
    *   **Complexity:** The paper introduces several components, which makes the approach more complex compared to simple monolithic architectures. However, the added complexity is justifiable given the improvements in performance, explainability, and generalization.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in VideoQA, particularly in the areas of causal reasoning, explainable AI, and modular model design. The CCE could be used as a standalone module in other applications beyond VideoQA. The causal chain dataset and CauCo metric will likely be adopted by other researchers in the field.

**Justification for the Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**. The paper demonstrates a significant advancement in Causal-Why VideoQA through its novel use of causal chains as explicit intermediate representations. It addresses critical limitations of existing monolithic approaches, leading to improved performance, explainability, and generalization. The contributions are well-motivated, rigorously evaluated, and have the potential to stimulate further research in the field. The weaknesses, while present, do not significantly diminish the overall value of the paper. It stands as a strong contribution pushing the boundaries of explainable and causally aware VideoQA.

### Inference-Time Alignment Control for Diffusion Models with Reinforcement Learning Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21016v1)
- **Authors**: Luozhijie Jin, Zijie Qiu, Jie Liu, Zijie Diao, Lifeng Qiao, Ning Ding, Alex Lamb, Xipeng Qiu
- **Abstract**: Denoising-based generative models, particularly diffusion and flow matching algorithms, have achieved remarkable success. However, aligning their output distributions with complex downstream objectives, such as human preferences, compositional accuracy, or data compressibility, remains challenging. While reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning methods, inspired by advances in RL from human feedback (RLHF) for large language models, have been adapted to these generative frameworks, current RL approaches are suboptimal for diffusion models and offer limited flexibility in controlling alignment strength after fine-tuning. In this work, we reinterpret RL fine-tuning for diffusion models through the lens of stochastic differential equations and implicit reward conditioning. We introduce Reinforcement Learning Guidance (RLG), an inference-time method that adapts Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG) by combining the outputs of the base and RL fine-tuned models via a geometric average. Our theoretical analysis shows that RLG's guidance scale is mathematically equivalent to adjusting the KL-regularization coefficient in standard RL objectives, enabling dynamic control over the alignment-quality trade-off without further training. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RLG consistently improves the performance of RL fine-tuned models across various architectures, RL algorithms, and downstream tasks, including human preferences, compositional control, compressibility, and text rendering. Furthermore, RLG supports both interpolation and extrapolation, thereby offering unprecedented flexibility in controlling generative alignment. Our approach provides a practical and theoretically sound solution for enhancing and controlling diffusion model alignment at inference. The source code for RLG is publicly available at the Github: https://github.com/jinluo12345/Reinforcement-learning-guidance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reinforcement Learning Guidance (RLG), a novel inference-time method for controlling the alignment of diffusion models with complex downstream objectives. RLG adapts Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG) by combining the outputs of a base and RL-fine-tuned model via a geometric average. The authors provide a theoretical analysis demonstrating that RLG's guidance scale is mathematically equivalent to adjusting the KL-regularization coefficient in standard RL objectives, enabling dynamic control over the alignment-quality trade-off without further training. Extensive experiments across various architectures, RL algorithms, and downstream tasks (including human preferences, compositional control, and data compressibility) validate RLG's effectiveness. RLG also supports interpolation and extrapolation, providing unprecedented flexibility in controlling generative alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of RLG – reinterpreting RL fine-tuning as reward conditioning and leveraging this to create an inference-time control mechanism – is relatively novel. The theoretical connection to KL-divergence control is also a valuable contribution. While CFG adaptations exist, RLG's specific approach and its grounding in RL theory differentiate it. The concurrent works of CFGRL and Diffusion Blend appear, which investigate inference time manipulation, but RLG focuses on the connection between interpolation and implicit reward guidance.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in generative modeling: aligning output distributions with complex, often subjective, objectives. The ability to dynamically control this alignment *without* retraining is a substantial advantage. The broad applicability of RLG across different models, RL methods, and tasks underscores its potential impact. The performance improvements demonstrated in the experiments provide empirical evidence of its practical value. RLG simplifies aligning with downstream objectives.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** The paper provides a solid theoretical justification for RLG, linking it to KL-divergence control and implicit reward conditioning. This distinguishes it from purely heuristic approaches.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering a diverse set of tasks and models. The quantitative results consistently demonstrate RLG's effectiveness.
    *   **Practical Applicability:** RLG is training-free and easy to implement, making it readily accessible to practitioners. Its compatibility with various models and RL methods further enhances its practicality.
    *   **Flexibility:** The interpolation/extrapolation capabilities of RLG offer unprecedented flexibility in controlling generative alignment, allowing users to dynamically balance competing objectives.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on CFG:** RLG inherits any limitations associated with CFG. CFG is known to have its limitations; further investigation in the mechanism of RLG under broad settings is warranted.

    *   **Assumption of Optimal RL Fine-tuning:** The theoretical justification relies on the assumption that the RL-fine-tuned model has converged to the optimal policy. While this is a common assumption, it may not always hold in practice. The sensitivity of RLG's performance to the quality of the RL-fine-tuned model should be explored.
*   **Potential Impact:** RLG has the potential to become a standard tool for controlling the alignment of diffusion models. Its ease of use and broad applicability could lead to significant advancements in various areas, including:

    *   **Human-centered generation:** Enabling users to fine-tune generative models to their specific preferences without retraining.
    *   **Content creation:** Facilitating precise control over image attributes, composition, and style.
    *   **Scientific applications:** Generating data that adheres to specific constraints and requirements.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel, theoretically grounded, and empirically validated method for controlling the alignment of diffusion models. Its ease of use, broad applicability, and flexibility make it a valuable contribution to the field. While the reliance on CFG and the assumption of optimal RL fine-tuning represent potential limitations, the overall impact of the paper is significant.
